Abstract: Trotterization is a widely used algorithm for simulating quantum evolutions and consists of approximating a target unitary through a local linear decomposition of its generator that is repeated over $N$ steps. We introduce {scheduled trotterization}, a variable-weight modification in which the coefficients of the local linear decomposition are allowed to change from step to step. Using the geometry of the Lie group of unitary operators, we express the global endpoint error as a product of transported local defects, whose leading terms are obtained by two applications of the Baker-Campbell-Hausdorff formula, one inside each local step and one between different transported defects, separating the internal Trotter error from the external accumulation error. While we recover recent tighter bounds on the global approximation error when the weights are held fixed, the new framework allows us to propose a weight-varying schedule that significantly decreases the global error. For exact infinitesimal synthesis, we prove that this improves the usual fixed-weight $O(N^{-1})$ behavior to $O(N^{-2})$, without changing the basic pulse skeleton. We also extend the framework to imperfect infinitesimal synthesis, as arises in quantum gate synthesis problems, where admissible coefficients may only approximate the desired local corrections. In this case, the error is controlled by a local covering parameter measuring the quality of the available coefficient set. Numerical experiments for random $\SU(2)$ targets further support the predicted theoretical improvements. The results suggest a novel perspective for the analysis of Trotter errors, which can be treated as schedulable quantities to be coherently canceled at the endpoint.

</details>

Abstract: Quantum devices can lose the resource needed for a specified function without entering a fixed failure subspace. We propose a resource-based reliability framework for this situation, quantifying the remaining task-relevant resource relative to a stated ideal capacity. The resource measure is selected through its operational connection to the task and the allowed operations. Abstract: We present a fully autonomous model of a mechanical Maxwell demon engine that converts heat absorbed by a qubit into population inversion in an attached quantum battery. A damped mechanical oscillator acts as a pointer that incorporates the essential demon function of information acquisition about the qubit state and subsequent feedback into the system dynamics, replacing external measurements and control operations. This is achieved by means of a Holstein-type Hamiltonian, which makes the pointer's equilibrium position depend on the qubit state and thereby induces a state-dependent resonance shift. The mechanism enables a selective energy transfer to an attached spin system that acts as the quantum battery. We derive both local and global Lindblad master equations for the coupled system and identify the parameter regimes in which the engine operates efficiently. The figure of merit is the resulting steady-state population inversion of the battery, quantified in terms of ergotropy. Based on these results, we discuss the necessary conditions for practical implementations of the demon engine on realistic experimental platforms.

Abstract: Variance squeezing of a V-type atom interacting with a dissipative cavity is investigated. First, the influence of atomic initial state parameters on the variance squeezing is explored. The results show that the atomic variance squeezing switches alternately between Sx and Sy as the initial state parameter alpha sweeps from -pi to pi. The two squeezing intervals are symmetric about alpha = pi/4, each with a width of pi/2. For alpha = pi/14, only the Sx component can generate variance squeezing and will eventually disappear with time evolution due to unavoidable environmental noise. Then, the influence of system parameters on the variance squeezing is also studied. It is found that, the optical variance squeezing depth of Sx is determined solely by the atomic initial parameters and is independent of the system parameters. Both weak cavity-environment coupling and atom-cavity detuning can significantly enhance the robustness of the Sx variance squeezing. The SGI parameter has an extremely weak influence on variance squeezing. These results are beneficial for high-precision quantum communication applications.

Abstract: Polynomial excitations consisting of creation and annihilation operators are studied. Subsystem entanglement is proven produced and preserved by such manipulations. NPT entanglement already present between any two subsystems of a general pure or mixed multipartite Gaussian state is preserved under all polynomial excitations. Nevertheless, these excitations can create NPT entanglement between initially separable subsystems, implying that the number of NPT entangled pairs cannot decrease. Polynomial excitations of a wide class of initially separable pure states are considered. For these states, the method reduces the infinite-dimensional positivity problem to a finite-dimensional one: after partial transposition, NPT entanglement is equivalent to a corresponding bilinear representation matrix not being positive semi-definite. Furthermore, using symplectic diagonalization and spectral decomposition, an explicit transformation is constructed between the Fock-space and position representations. It is applied to quadratic excitations of a three-mode mixed Gaussian state. The behavior of the NPT entanglement is tracked between subsystems as a function of a parameter $κ$, which tunes the non-locality of the excitation, and quantum negativity is evaluated. Under a chosen quadratic Hamiltonian, the resulting unitary time evolution maintains NPT entanglement for long intervals, interrupted only by short periods of PPT states. Finally, a closed analytical expression is derived for the covariance matrix of a general quadratic operator for an arbitrary number of degrees of freedom in phase space. Our framework directly connects mathematical foundations, theoretical modeling, and experimentally feasible implementations.

Abstract: The ergotropy, or unitarily extractable work, of a quantum system naturally splits into incoherent and coherent contributions, respectively set by population inversion and coherences in the energy eigenbasis. We show that this split makes the coherent ergotropy of a Mach--Zehnder qubit sensitive to path predictability that the output interference fringes do not reveal. We send the path qubit, prepared as a thermal quantum battery, through two quantum-controlled interferometers built from the same elements, a fixed beam splitter and a beam splitter coherently controlled to be present or absent, differing only in the order in which these two act. The two arrangements share identical visibility, stored energy, and incoherent ergotropy at every point in the parameter space. Yet, for work extracted from the output battery alone, placing the controlled beam splitter first never yields less coherent ergotropy, and at fixed visibility and interferometric phase the difference is governed by the path predictability that this ordering creates. The same expression reappears as a purity deficit on the beam-splitter controller, so the work advantage comes with a Landauer bound on the cost of locally resetting the controller that is never larger at the same temperature. We further examine the effects of decoherence on the battery and consider a relativistic realization in which a Wigner rotation correlates the particle's spin with its path. Tracing out the spin decoheres the battery and allows the two arrangements to exhibit different coherent ergotropies even in regimes where their non-relativistic counterparts coincide. These results show that interference visibility is not a complete measure of the coherence available as work.

Abstract: Quantum computing promises new capabilities beyond classical computation. Mid-circuit measurements (MCMs) enable error correction and outcome-dependent control, but their reliability is limited by noise on superconducting hardware. These operations depend on accurate quantum state discrimination (QSD), which assigns measured signals to state labels. Neural network-based (NN-based) QSD improves this assignment, yet its error remains bounded below by the Bayes risk of the available readout distributions. A complementary challenge is state deterioration as successive measurements accumulate and circuit execution advances, making static calibration less representative of the workload. Encoding supplies joint in-phase and quadrature (I-Q) readout signals from the target and auxiliary qubits, while contextual calibration trains the decoder for the conditions encountered during execution. Abstract: In order to execute quantum algorithms on bosonic quantum computers, it is necessary to encode their inputs into a desired target quantum state, with a process called state preparation. In real settings, state preparation methods operate in stochastic scenarios, where the prepared state is represented as a mixed state, that is a probabilistic ensemble of pure states. Therefore, existing state preparation methods optimize a variational ansatz by averaging over all possible sequences of photon loss events contained in the prepared ensemble, yielding an output state which approximates the desired target with a certain average fidelity. Such an approach, however, does not take into account directly the distribution of fidelities across states in the ensemble. In this work, we aim to maximize the fidelity of only a subset of pure states sampled from the prepared ensemble, at the cost of reducing the average fidelity overall. This trade-off is relevant when the goal is to obtain high-fidelity states with a certain probability, rather than maximizing the average fidelity of the prepared ensemble. We borrow and adapt objective functions from risk-sensitive optimization, a framework that optimizes specific characteristics of a distribution rather than only its expectation, and that has been successfully applied in Reinforcement Learning. In the considered experimental settings, our results show that the infidelities of some pure states in the prepared ensemble can be reduced by up to four orders of magnitude when using risk-seeking objectives, compared to those achieved using the standard optimization of the average fidelity. Furthermore, we show that it is possible to control the trade-off between the probability of obtaining high-fidelity states and the lowest infidelity threshold reached, providing the flexibility to select the operating point that best suits a certain application.

</details>

Abstract: We consider a closed macroscopic quantum system in a pure state $ψ$ evolving unitarily with Hamiltonian $H$ in a finite-dimensional Hilbert space $\mathscr{H}$. Following von Neumann, we assume a decomposition of $\mathscr{H}$ into mutually orthogonal subspaces $\mathscr{H}_ν$, where each $ν$ represents a macro state, and $\mathscr{H}_ν$ contains the pure states compatible with $ν$. We define the quantum Boltzmann entropy for a macro state $ν$ as $S_\mathrm{qB}(ν) = k_{\mathrm{B}} \log \dim \mathscr{H}_ν$, where $k_\mathrm{B}$ is the Boltzmann constant. Goldstein et al. Abstract: We investigate how unresolved quasistatic disorder in system bath coupling affects non-Markovian dynamics in spin environments. The coupling is fixed within each experimental shot but varies between shots, so repeated shot tomography reconstructs a disorder averaged dynamical map. For a single spin bath with pure dephasing Ising interaction, disorder can drive a transition to CP-divisible dynamics for fixed bath preparations, with a critical strength set by bath polarization along the interaction axis. However, when the bath state fluctuates physically between shots, the averaged dynamics remain CP indivisible at any finite disorder strength. We extend these results analytically to inhomogeneously coupled pure dephasing baths of any number \(N\) of non-interacting spins. At any finite disorder strength, one unpolarized bath spin suffices to retain CP-indivisible dynamics, whereas a fully polarized bath yields Markovian (CP-divisible) dynamics. Dynamics with physically fluctuating bath states likewise remain non-Markovian (CP indivisible) at finite disorder for arbitrary \(N\) in the pure dephasing case. For anisotropic Heisenberg interactions, any nonzero transverse exchange eliminates the disorder driven transition to CP divisibility in a single spin bath. Exact diagonalization of anisotropic models with inhomogeneous couplings up to \(N=12\) reveals persistent trace distance revivals for fixed bath preparations, sampled Haar random preparations, and physically fluctuating bath states, showing that this effect of transverse exchange extends beyond a single bath spin. Increasing disorder still suppresses experimentally accessible revivals and can make memory effects unresolvable at finite measurement resolution even as the dynamics remain CP indivisible. Thus, transverse exchange and physical bath state fluctuations preserve dynamical indivisibility against unresolved coupling disorder.

</details>

Abstract: Explicitly correlated methods, which represent dominant short-range correlations by a diagonal Jastrow-type correlator, are designed to accurately describe systems of strongly correlated electrons, such as the fully periodic 2D Fermi--Hubbard model. However, as the reference state is compact in momentum space whereas the correlator acts naturally in real space, there is a fundamental mismatch of bases that classical algorithms resolve only by restricting the reference or the correlator. Here we propose a hybrid algorithm in which a quantum processor prepares a low-entanglement momentum-space matrix product state (MPS), precomputed classically by the transcorrelated method at the cost of a nonvariational energy, rotates it to real space, and measures in bases fixed by the Hamiltonian. Because the measurement bases are independent of the correlator, the correlator can be variationally optimized entirely in classical post-processing with no quantum-classical feedback loop. We demonstrate our algorithm on 32 qubits of Quantinuum's H2-2 with a bond-dimension-4 MPS for the half-filled $4 \times 4$ lattice and achieve a relative ground-state energy error of $16 \pm 4\%$ after postselection, reduced to $3.5 \pm 0.5\%$ by calibration using low-order moments from the MPS. Compared with other feedback-free hybrid algorithms, even under generous assumptions, our method requires fewer samples than quantum-selected configuration interaction and fewer two-qubit gates than quantum Krylov methods.

Abstract: We introduce PULSUS, a direct frequency-domain framework for multidimensional spectroscopy of open quantum systems beyond the impulsive rotating-wave approximation. Coherence-frequency intervals are represented through Liouvillian resolvents, while finite Gaussian pulses are incorporated through operator-valued functions of the field-free Liouvillian. This enables direct evaluation on Cartesian grids or arbitrary spectral coordinates while retaining molecular evolution during the pulse envelopes and, when required, the complete light--matter interaction.
  The implementation is validated against analytical and independent time-domain benchmarks and supports targeted spectral sampling that reduces the number of evaluated coordinates when the response is spectrally localized. A controlled hierarchy of finite-pulse, rotating-wave, short-pulse, and impulsive models separates the errors introduced by each approximation. For a coupled electronic dimer, the total impulsive-RWA error is nonmonotonic in pulse duration: an intermediate regime provides the highest accuracy, whereas ultrashort pulses expose coupling-enabled beyond-RWA pathways and longer pulses develop substantial finite-pulse corrections. These results show that the impulsive and rotating-wave approximations cannot be assumed to improve simultaneously and should be tested when quantitatively interpreting multidimensional spectra. PULSUS provides a direct frequency-domain framework for carrying out that comparison.

</details>

Abstract: We study quantum communication, entanglement distribution, and secret-key generation through multimode bosonic loss and fading channels, assisted by local quantum operations and two-way classical communication. Using sector teleportation, we determine the exact two-way quantum and secret-key capacities of multimode pure-loss channels under a mean photon-number constraint, allowing arbitrary adaptive quantum protocols. We extend these results to continuous frequency-dependent loss under a power constraint and identify the optimal allocation of photons among optical modes. For constant loss over an unlimited bandwidth, we obtain a closed-form capacity proportional to the square root of power. For randomly varying loss, we determine exact finite-energy capacities when the channel realization is publicly revealed, distinguishing knowledge before and after transmission. When fading remains unobserved, we derive capacity bounds and explicit formulas for an on/off channel. We also establish strong-converse bounds, with exact thresholds for the deterministic loss models considered here, observed fading, and unobserved fading with finitely many possible transmissivities. Under mean energy constraints, these thresholds generally exceed the corresponding finite-energy capacities. These results guide energy allocation and provide benchmarks for energy-constrained optical quantum communication with multimode signals and variable loss.

</details>

Abstract: Massive mechanical oscillators couple directly to forces, accelerations and gravity, making them attractive probes of new physics and of quantum mechanics at macroscopic scales. Pushing sensitivities beyond the standard quantum limit for signals of unknown phase requires non-Gaussian quantum states, whose metrological advantage has so far been confined to lighter systems such as trapped ions. Here we operate a microgram-scale acoustic mode of a high-overtone bulk acoustic wave resonator as a phase-insensitive quantum-enhanced sensor. A transmon qubit prepares the mode in Fock states of up to seven phonons by optimal control, and resolves its phonon-number distribution after interaction with a weak resonant signal. Bayesian analysis yields a Fisher information five times the vacuum value, a 7.0 dB gain in signal-amplitude sensitivity over any classical probe state, irrespective of its energy, currently limited by state preparation infidelities. We then train a neural network on the measured sensor response to perform Bayesian estimation without any analytical model of the system, showing the same sensitivity gain with half the systematic bias. Our results establish acoustic resonators prepared in non-Gaussian states as quantum-enhanced probes for high-frequency gravitational waves, dark matter and wave-function collapse.

</details>

Abstract: Multimode mechanical resonators offer a large set of densely packed, long-lived bosonic modes within a single compact device, making them attractive for quantum simulation, quantum information processing, and quantum-enhanced sensing. Many such applications rely on multipartite entanglement, yet deterministic mechanical entanglement has so far been largely limited to pairs of modes. Here we demonstrate the deterministic and programmable preparation of multipartite entangled states among the phonon modes of a high-overtone bulk acoustic wave resonator coupled to a superconducting transmon qubit. By sequentially applying optimal-control-engineered isometries between the qubit and successive modes, we generate matrix product states of bond dimension two, including $W$, Greenberger-Horne-Zeilinger (GHZ), and cluster states. We fully characterize these states via quantum state tomography and certify genuine multipartite entanglement among up to four modes. We further demonstrate correlated-noise sensing by exploiting the complementary decoherence responses of the stored $W$ and GHZ states, isolating inter-mode phase correlations from local decoherence and placing quantitative bounds on collective dephasing. Our results establish a scalable approach to preparing multipartite entangled states in mechanical systems, with promising applications in multiparameter sensing and tests of quantum mechanics with massive objects.

</details>

Abstract: We develop and analyze an entropic descent method for a nonconvex optimization problem on probability simplices arising in classical and quantum secure communication. The optimization variables describe a randomized encoder, allowing its input distribution and stochastic preprocessing to be optimized jointly. An exact relative-entropy identity yields a channel-independent majorization bound and an explicit mirror-descent iteration with a fixed unit step size. We establish quantitative descent, convergence of objective values, and stationarity of accumulation points on their support faces for arbitrary finite classical channels, without assuming degradedness or less noisiness. For strictly positive transition matrices, a square-root parametrization and a Kurdyka--Łojasiewicz argument establish finite length of the transformed trajectory and convergence of the entire sequence, including limits on the simplex boundary. For less-noisy channels, a restricted convex formulation admits global convergence with an explicit \(O(1/t)\) objective-error bound. We extend the relative-entropy construction to noncommuting quantum outputs and derive monotone alternating updates for covariant encoders, including correlated preprocessing over a fixed block. Numerical experiments compare the method with analytic benchmarks and constrained optimization, reveal multiple attracting values, and demonstrate strict improvements from stochastic preprocessing.

</details>

Abstract: A series of recent works has established EFI pairs as the "minimal" assumption for quantum cryptography. Recently, Cavalar et al. [CCC+26] showed that EFI pairs are equivalent to non-uniform expanding single-copy pseudorandom state generators (1PRS), and obtained a non-uniform meta-complexity characterization of EFI pairs. We remove the non-uniformity in their construction and prove that EFI pairs are equivalent to expanding 1PRS. As a corollary, we obtain the first uniform meta-complexity characterization of EFI pairs. Together with the equivalence of statistically verifiable one-way state generators and commitments proved by Batra and Jain [BJ24], this gives a quantum analogue of the classical equivalence between one-way functions, commitments, and pseudorandom generators.

</details>

Abstract: It is proved that every Borel ensemble of pure states on a separable bipartite Hilbert space with finite average entanglement admits a countable ensemble with the same barycenter and arbitrarily small additional average entanglement. Consequently, the discrete and continuous versions of the Entanglement of Formation coincide on the whole space of states of infinite-dimensional bipartite quantum system (with infinite values allowed).
  The main idea of the proof (realized with the AI-assistance) is the identification of a Borel ensemble of pure bipartite states with a Borel probability measure on the unit sphere of the space of Hilbert--Schmidt operators followed by dividing the support of this measure into appropriate cells and replacing each cell by its conditional barycenter. The resulting countable ensemble is obtained by a recursive construction.

</details>

Abstract: Loss and noise limit the use of light to distribute entanglement, transmit quantum information, and establish secret keys. Gaussian states have a compact description in terms of means and covariance matrices, but their entanglement still requires global optimization. We develop a framework for computing Gaussian-restricted versions of the relative entropy of entanglement and Rains entanglement. An integral representation reveals a convex geometry under matrix geometric means and reduces Gaussian Rains entanglement to relative entropy minimized over Gaussian states with positive partial transpose. We obtain unique optimal reference states and additivity for independent Gaussian input states. Our convex-majorization algorithm has an explicit geometric convergence rate, uniform in the faithful regularization parameter and valid for pure input states. Controlled numerical integration and approximate convex subproblem solutions give a bit-complexity bound that is polynomial in accuracy bits for each fixed rational input covariance matrix, with explicit dependence on covariance size. These calculations bound extractable entanglement and, through the relative entropy of entanglement, extractable secret key. Teleportation simulation, energy allocation, and a convex optimization of passive-basis loss cores give bounds on quantum and private communication under an input-energy constraint, allowing arbitrary two-way classically assisted protocols. Two- and three-mode examples compare the bounds with achievable rates, including matching pure-loss benchmarks. Finally, we separate the Gaussian-restricted quantities from their unrestricted counterparts and give a photon-number truncation method with controlled error for computing the latter.

</details>

Abstract: Many quantum gravity theories predict spacetime fluctuations at the Planck scale. Neutrinos, which interact only via the weak force and gravity, propagate over long distances without losing quantum coherence. Planck-scale fluctuations would spoil that coherence, damping the flavor oscillation probability over long baselines. This makes neutrinos sensitive interferometers for Planck-scale physics. We present a search for quantum decoherence using 368,071 atmospheric neutrinos detected by IceCube in the energy range 0.5-100 TeV, an order of magnitude higher in energy than any previous decoherence search. We find no evidence for decoherence and set the strongest constraints to date on this class of neutrino decoherence models, improving on previous limits by over an order of magnitude for decoherence rate scaling as $E^2$ and $E^3$. These results exclude natural Planck-suppressed decoherence for all energy scalings $E^n$ with $0\leq n \leq 3$, the first such exclusion at the Planck scale for $n=3$.

Abstract: This Letter presents the first measurement of the $B^{0} \to K^{+}π^{-}μ^{+}μ^{-}$ differential branching fraction in the region of the dihadron $K^+π^-$ mass, $m_{Kπ}$, $1000< m_{Kπ} < 1835\,\text{MeV}/c^{2}$. This decay mode is mediated by the quark-level transition $b \to s\ell^{+}\ell^{-}$, for which the measured effective couplings show tensions with Standard Model predictions. The analysis is performed using proton-proton collision data collected with the LHCb detector between 2011 and 2018, corresponding to an integrated luminosity of $8.4\,\text{fb}^{-1}$. The differential branching fraction is determined across different regions of dimuon mass squared, $q^2$, in the interval $0.1 < q^2 < 18.0\,\text{GeV}^2/c^{4}$, as well as in different regions of $ m_{Kπ}$ in the interval $1.1 < q^2 < 6.0\,\text{GeV}^2/c^{4}$. The results represent the first differential measurement of the branching fraction across $m_{Kπ}$ within the selected dihadron-mass region, and the first observation of the decay within this region above the $J/ψ$ resonance.

</details>

Abstract: Energy-energy correlators probe perturbative and nonperturbative QCD regimes of jet formation through momentum-weighted angular correlations between hadrons. First measurements in light-quark-enriched jets are presented for inclusive, like-sign, and opposite-sign charged-hadron pairs, using proton-proton collisions at LHCb. The results support universality of the confinement scale for light-quark-initiated jets across collision systems and the reported transverse momentum scale of the jet. In the charge-dependent measurements, opposite-sign correlations are enhanced as angular separation decreases down to the confinement-scale region, a behavior that is well described by simulations.

</details>


### [174] [Forward decay chain reconstruction with displaced vertices in intense magnetic fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14666)
*Sergio Javier Jaimes Elles,Simone Libralon,Fernando Martinez Vidal,Nicola Neri,Miguel Rebollo de Miguel,Izaac Sanderswood,Volodymyr Svintozelskyi,Giorgia Tonani,Javier Velilla Serna,Ziyi Wang*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: Summary generation failed


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Motivation analysis unavailable

Method: Method extraction failed

Result: Result analysis unavailable

Conclusion: Conclusion extraction failed

Abstract: Precise reconstruction of particle decay chains is essential for a broad range of analyses in particle physics experiments, particularly those targeting flavour physics and spectroscopy. We present a standalone, modular and extensible decay tree reconstruction framework designed to handle complicated topologies, accounting for tightly constrained particle decays and long-distance trajectory extrapolations through regions of intense and inhomogeneous magnetic field, as well as limited momentum resolution. Implemented in C++, the framework is optimised for configurability and performance. Developed primarily within CERN's LHCb experiment, its focus is on decay chains with vertices located several metres from the LHC proton-proton interaction point, where the dipole magnetic field is maximal and non-uniform. Even so, it remains flexible: it can accommodate these special cases as well as the approximations commonly used in standard decay tree fitting tools, and the geometries of general-purpose collider detectors. Benchmark studies using simulated data demonstrate significant improvements in reconstruction efficiency, invariant mass resolution, and vertex position resolution with respect to existing tools, opening new opportunities for precision measurements and searches involving highly displaced decays.

</details>


### [175] [Exploring Collective Effects in Small Collision Systems via Light-Flavour Particle Production in High-Multiplicity pp Collisions at $\sqrt{s}=$ 13 TeV in ALICE](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14851)
*Rajendra Nath Patra for the ALICE Collaboration*

Abstract: High-multiplicity pp and p-Pb collisions show signatures of radial and anisotropic flow, as well as the enhanced production of strange hadrons. These observations resemble phenomena seen in heavy-ion collisions, where a quark-gluon plasma (QGP) is formed, although their origin in small systems remains under discussion. This contribution presents ALICE Run 2 results on light-flavour hadron ($π$, K, p) production in high-multiplicity (HM) pp collisions at $\sqrt{s}=13$ TeV, where the charged-particle multiplicity reaches values comparable to peripheral or semi-peripheral Pb-Pb collisions. The multiplicity dependence of $π$, K, and p transverse momentum spectra, $p_{\rm T}$-differential and $p_{\rm T}$-integrated particle ratios, and mean transverse momenta ($\langle p_{\rm T}\rangle$) is presented and compared with published results from other collision systems and energies, as well as with PYTHIA 8 and EPOS4 model calculations.

</details>


### [176] [Detecting photons in radiative Bhabha interaction with Si-strip and LYSO crystals at future e+e- colliders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15036)
*Chentao Bao,Hai Chen,Wang Chen,Zhujun Fang,Jiading Gong,Suen Hou,Xiaolin Kang,Mingyi Liu,Ming Qi,Liqing Qin,Weimin Song,Jie Wang,Yadi Wang,Lei Zhang,Linghua Zhang*

Abstract: Next-generation e+e-colliders have been proposed for the Super Tau Charm Facility covering center-of-mass energies sqrt(s) = 2-7 GeV, and for the Higgs Factories optimized for Z-pole physics and for HZ production. Luminosity can be determined by measuring forward Bhabha scattering. The QED cross-section calculation has reached a precision of better than 10^-3. Detecting radiative photons in Bhabha events provides a tool for validating higher-order corrections in theory. We propose the Forward Detector for detecting Bhabha scattering events at the STCF. The design uses high-resolution silicon detectors and finely segmented LYSO crystals to discriminate radiative photons from closely associated electrons. Bhabha scattering events are simulated with the BHLUMI and BHWIDE programs, and the detector is modeled using GEANT. Systematic uncertainties due to multiple scattering and bremsstrahlung in the beam pipe were evaluated. The potential for achieving precise measurements of radiative processes in Bhabha events is investigated.

</details>

Abstract: Using $(2712.4\pm14.3)\times10^{6}$ $ψ(3686)$ events collected with the BESIII detector at the BEPCII collider, we report the first observations of the decay $χ_{cJ}(J=0,1,2) \to Σ^0\barΣ^0η$. The signal significances, after considering systematic uncertainties, are determined to be 7.2$σ$, 5.7$σ$, and 6.7$σ$ for $χ_0$, $χ_{c1}$, and $χ_{c2}$, respectively. The branching fractions are measured to be $\mathcal B(χ_{c0}\toΣ^0\barΣ^0η)=({9.7 \pm 1.7 \pm 0.8}) \times 10^{-5}$, $\mathcal B(χ_{c1}\toΣ^0\barΣ^0η)=({4.9 \pm 1.1 \pm 0.4}) \times 10^{-5}$, and $\mathcal B(χ_{c2}\toΣ^0\barΣ^0η)=({6.1 \pm 1.2 \pm 0.3}) \times 10^{-5}$, where the first uncertainties are statistical and the second systematic. No significant structure in the $Σ^0\barΣ^0,Σ^0η,\barΣ^0η$ mass distributions is observed under the current statistics.

</details>

Abstract: Using a data sample corresponding to an integrated luminosity of 43.8~fb$^{-1}$ collected with the \mbox{BESIII} detector at 44 center-of-mass energy points between 3.510 and 4.946~GeV, the Born cross sections for the process $e^+e^- \to p \bar{n} π^-$ and its charge-conjugated process are measured. The energy dependence of the dressed cross sections is investigated to search for possible contributions from vector charmonium and charmonium-like states. No evidence for contributions from the $ψ(3770)$, $Y(4230)$, $Y(4360)$, $ψ(4415)$, $Y(4500)$, or $Y(4710)$ resonances is observed. The upper limits at the 90\% confidence level are determined for the products of the electronic partial widths and the branching fractions for the decays of these resonances into the $p \bar{n} π^- + \mathrm{c.c.}$ final state.

</details>

Abstract: The feasibility of real-time track fitting of straight tracks for online event selection is shown in the context of the MUonE experiment. In order to reach the high throughput and low latency required for real-time track fitting, the tracking algorithm is implemented using High-Level Synthesis for an FPGA, which provides provides a balance between flexibility and performance. Hardware tracking results from a test with high-rate muons at the CERN M2 beam are presented. This test, a joint effort by the MUonE and Compact Muon Solenoid (CMS) experiments, operated with Two-Strip (2S) modules provided by the CMS tracker group. The prototype version of the fitter used in this test achieves 3.75 $μ$s latency and 7.75 % resource use. A comparison of the results with those obtained from a pure software implementation shows agreement well below relevant uncertainty scales. Developments since the test are also presented, with the hardware algorithm now reaching 1.35 $μ$s latency and producing pipelined fitting results above 50 MHz while requiring less resources than the initial version. Doing so required including certain assumptions in the algorithm, but these assumptions hold for linear track fitting in general and algorithm remains useful in contexts other than MUonE.

</details>

*Zichun Hao,Raghav Kansal,Abhijith Gandrakota,Chang Sun,Haoyang Li,Zihan Zhao,Ngadiuba Jennifer,Javier Duarte,Maria Spiropulu*

Abstract: Supervised machine learning in high-energy physics (HEP) requires training on labeled simulations of particle collisions, which are known to be unfaithful to the underlying physics. This leads to significant inaccuracies and biases in the transfer of trained models to real data, limiting the power of deep learning methods. Self-supervised learning (SSL) is a promising alternative for pretraining directly on real, unlabeled data. However, existing techniques largely operate with inductive biases inspired by domains such as images and natural language. We propose RINO (Renormalization Group DIstillation with NO Labels), which, for the first time, exploits the fundamentally multi-scale renormalization group structure of high-energy jet formation as a physically grounded source of SSL views. We define teacher-student representations across energy scales using different levels of hierarchical clustering, aligned via DINO-style self-distillation. To mirror the background-dominated nature of real collider data, RINO pretrains exclusively on jets from quantum chromodynamics (QCD) processes and is evaluated on out-of-distribution (OOD) transfer to rare signal classes. In our controlled data-efficiency study, RINO matches fully supervised training on JetNet after finetuning on only 1% of the labeled data. With the full labeled dataset, RINO improves OOD classification accuracy on top-quark jets by 5.9% and, without retraining, improves AUC on the unseen Higgs boson decay channel $H \to 4q$ by 4.1% over the strongest SSL baseline. Scale-structure diagnostics reveal that DINO-style self-distillation permits our backbone to retain scale-dependent structure. Ablation studies show that this accounts for an 8.6% OOD accuracy gap with respect to alternative SSL paradigms such as SimCLR. Code is available at https://github.com/zichunhao/RINO.

</details>

Abstract: High-pileup conditions in CMS during the HL-LHC era make charged-particle tracking increasingly challenging as detector occupancy and combinatorics grow. We present a hybrid approach that exploits Line Segment Tracking (LST) objects rather than individual hits to enable the first CMS ML-based track reconstruction algorithm. The LST objects, namely the triplets used in this study, are built according to geometry- and physics-driven criteria and carry richer local structure, geometrical cues, and physics quantities such as transverse momentum, which helps the model incorporate detector-level information from the start. We apply a Transformer encoder with geometrically masked sparse self-attention to embed these objects in a learned latent space, and use object condensation to assemble full tracks in a single inference step. This object-based representation reduces complexity in dense environments and makes the model less sensitive to ambiguities introduced by high pileup. Evaluated on CMS Phase-2 simulation of $t\bar{t}$ events at $\langle μ\rangle = 200$, the approach achieves reconstruction efficiency comparable to the LST quintuplet baseline while reducing the fake rate by roughly a factor of three and suppressing duplicates almost entirely, demonstrating the promise of advanced ML architectures for next-generation particle tracking.

</details>

Abstract: The increasing event complexity and data rates foreseen for future LHC coinditions motivate a transition from selecting proton-proton ($pp$) collision events by reconstructing predefined signal candidates to constructing a reduced data stream by interpreting the relevant event features in a signal-agnostic way. We explore the capabilities of deep-learning-based architectures on heavy-flavour physics using LHCb as a case study. Graph-based full-event interpretation techniques address the problem by representing visible products of $pp$ collisions as nodes of a graph, and reconstructing heavy-hadron decay chains hierarchically. A new neutral-particle-aware architecture extends the input data from charged tracks to calorimeter clusters, and uses metric learning to clusterize particles from the same decay chain in a common latent space. Tests on inclusive simulated $pp\to b\bar b$ events show that this approach retains its ability to reconstruct decay chains across a broad range of topologies and multiplicities. We discuss the resulting physics opportunities, with a focus on inclusive $b\to s\ell\ell$ measurements and on searches for exotic hadrons.

</details>

Abstract: Muons have fascinated physicists since their discovery in cosmic rays almost a century ago. Their large mass and relatively long lifetime allow relativistic muons to traverse large amounts of matter, a property that has been exploited to probe otherwise inaccessible structures, from volcanoes and archaeological monuments to shielded nuclear materials. Here, we experimentally demonstrate for the first time that muon scattering tomography can be extended to biological soft tissue. We image an anthropomorphic thorax phantom using a 2 GeV/c muon beam at the CERN Proton Synchrotron and reconstruct its three-dimensional scattering density from the measured deflections of individual muons. Despite the weak scattering produced by tissue-equivalent materials, the reconstructed density agrees with the expectation from simulation at the percent level. Our results extend muon imaging into a previously unexplored regime and open the possibility of using muons as a complementary probe for medical imaging.

</details>

Abstract: Tests of lepton-flavour universality in semileptonic \(B\)-meson decays provide sensitive probes of physics beyond the Standard Model. The Belle and Belle II experiments, respectively operating on the KEKB and SuperKEKB colliders in Japan, have collected \(e^+e^-\to B\bar{B}\) collisions at a center-of-mass energy corresponding to the \(Υ(4S)\) resonance in samples with a combined integrated luminosity exceeding \(1\,\mathrm{ab}^{-1}\). These data, with low particle multiplicity, constrained initial-state kinematics, and excellent lepton identification, provide an ideal environment to study lepton-flavour universality in semileptonic decays of \(B\) mesons. New results on the ratios of semitauonic \(B\)-decay rates compared to analogous light-lepton rates are presented. These include new Belle II measurements of branching-fraction ratios \(R(D^{(*)})\) involving exclusive \(B\to D^{(*)}τν\) and \(B\to D^{(*)}\ellν\) decays using semileptonic or hadronic reconstruction of the accompanying \(B\)-meson tag. Belle II and Belle measurements of angular observables in \(B\to D^*\ellν\) modes that test light-lepton universality and the Lorentz structure of the decay are also presented. The measurements are statistically limited and individually consistent with Standard Model expectations. The new \(R(D^{(*)})\) results are also compatible with experimental world averages, which continue to exhibit tension with Standard Model predictions. Belle II has now accumulated an \(Υ(4S)\) data sample exceeding that of Belle, offering substantial opportunities for new analysis approaches and heightened precision.

</details>

Abstract: We present a real-time trackless method to monitor the centre of the LHCb luminous region and the relative alignment of the two halves of the LHCb Vertex Locator, using cluster counts accumulated in selected regions of the detector and processed in firmware. A Principal Component Analysis performed on simulated data yields a set of weight vectors that reduce the position estimators to scalar products of the counter rates, executable with negligible computational overhead. With pp collision data from 2024, the transverse position of the luminous region is reconstructed with a statistical precision of approximately 4 micrometre per coordinate in a 90 millisecond accumulation window. A measurement of the longitudinal centre is demonstrated with a statistical precision of a few millimetres in simulated studies. The introduction of run-time configurable firmware counters has been studied in simulation to improve the transverse and longitudinal resolutions by a factor of three with respect to the fixed-geometry baseline.

Abstract: A model-independent amplitude analysis of the $π^{0} π^{0}$ system produced in radiative $ψ(3686)$ decays is presented. The analysis is based on the unique dataset of $(2712.4\pm14.3)\times10^6$ $ψ(3686)$ decays collected by the BESIII detector. Scalar and tensor partial-wave contributions are obtained as piecewise functions of $M_{\text{inv}}(π^{0} π^{0})$ with minimal model assumptions. The observed contribution of the scalar partial wave exhibits similar behavior to the one reported in the study of radiative $J/ψ$ decays, while the tensor contributions provide no evidence for resonance-like structures except for the clearly visible $f_2(1270)$. The results are presented in the form of log-likelihood scans in $M_{\text{inv}}(π^{0} π^{0})$ bins, which preserve all the statistical information and account for relevant systematic uncertainties. The model-independent description provided in this paper allows to combine the presented results with other related data in the development of phenomenological models.

</details>


### [188] [Probing the proton structure with minimum bias and low-pt dimuon events at hadron colliders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18685)
*Kyle Cormier*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: Summary generation failed


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Motivation analysis unavailable

Method: Method extraction failed

Result: Result analysis unavailable

Conclusion: Conclusion extraction failed

Abstract: This paper presents two methods for probing the proton structure and the number of partonic interactions in proton-proton collisions using dimuon production and minimum bias events. At the LHC these targeted measurements can provide information on the average number of partonic scatters in different event classes, their dependence on the partonic momentum fraction, x, and potentially partonic flavour. Within a simple mostly geometric model of multiparton interactions as quasi-independent scatters, the measurements can be used to make inference about the proton-proton overlap dependence on impact parameter and its partonic-x dependence.

</details>
